| <a id="board-py-L17"></a>17 | <code>EMPTY, PAWN, KNIGHT, BISHOP, ROOK, QUEEN, KING = range(7)  # Piece kinds</code> | `range(7)` supplies 0 through 6; assignment unpacks them into EMPTY=0, PAWN=1, KNIGHT=2, BISHOP=3, ROOK=4, QUEEN=5, and KING=6. These are piece kinds, not material values. Every engine module agrees on this numbering, so a move's promotion value can use the same codes. |
| <a id="board-py-L18"></a>18 | <code>WHITE = 8  # Added to the kind of a White piece</code> | The color flag is 8, binary `1000`. Add it to kinds 1 through 6 to obtain White pieces 9 through 14. Black keeps kinds 1 through 6. Because the low three bits hold the kind, `piece & 7` removes this color flag and `piece & WHITE` keeps it. The value 8 alone is not a valid piece. |
| <a id="board-py-L19"></a>19 | <code>MATE_SCORE = 100_000  # Larger than any material count</code> | Sets the shared mate-score magnitude to 100,000. Search uses it for terminal scores, and the transposition table uses it to recognize scores that need mate-distance normalization. |
| <a id="board-py-L20"></a>20 | <em>blank</em> | Blank line separating related statements, definitions, or paragraphs for readability. It introduces no object, branch, board change, or search operation. |
| <a id="board-py-L21"></a>21 | <code>Position = tuple&#91;list&#91;int&#93;, bool, int, int&#93;</code> | Defines the type alias `Position`: a tuple containing a mutable list of piece integers, a Boolean side-to-move flag, castling-right bits, and an en passant square. Type hints help readers and type checkers; they do not validate positions at runtime. The tuple does not make its nested board list immutable. |
| <a id="board-py-L22"></a>22 | <code>Move = tuple&#91;int, int, int&#93;  # (from square, to square, promotion or 0)</code> | Defines `Move` as three integers: source square, destination square, and promotion kind. `(52, 36, 0)` means e2-e4; `(12, 4, 5)` means e7-e8 promoting to a queen. A normal move uses 0 in the final slot, and captures need no separate flag because the position supplies the captured piece. |

#### Lines 40 to 44: is_own()

| Line | Exact source | Detailed explanation |

| <a id="board-py-L40"></a>40 | <code>def is_own(piece: int, white: bool) -&gt; bool:</code> | Defines a helper whose inputs are an encoded piece and the color to test. The result is Boolean. Here `white` means 'does this piece belong to White?' rather than always meaning the position's side to move; callers can pass `not white` to ask about an enemy piece. |
| <a id="board-py-L41"></a>41 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;"""Return True if the piece belongs to White (white is True) or Black."""</code> | One-line docstring for is_own. It records the function or class contract as text; the following executable lines implement that contract. A docstring does not validate inputs or enforce its stated guarantee. |
| <a id="board-py-L42"></a>42 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;return piece &gt; WHITE if white else EMPTY &lt; piece &lt; WHITE</code> | Uses a conditional expression: for White, accept piece values greater than 8; for Black, accept values strictly between 0 and 8. Empty=0 fails both branches. For valid pieces, 10 belongs to White and 2 to Black. This assumes valid encoding: it does not reject every out-of-range integer. |

This is a hash, not a collision-proof identity: the cache stores no full position for comparison. The raw en passant square is retained even if no capture is available. |

To attack a target, a White pawn must stand one row below it, at `(row+1, col-1)` or `(row+1, col+1)`; Black uses one row above. These are opposite to the pawn's forward movement direction because the search begins at the attacked square. |

For a rook direction this is one row away or the same row; for a knight it can be two rows away. |

Python evaluates `and` left to right, so it checks bounds before indexing board. Non-sliders skip the loop entirely and test only their single candidate square below. |
| <a id="attacks-py-L56"></a>56 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;r += row_change</code> | Advances the row along the ray. A horizontal direction has row_change=0, so the row stays fixed while the next line advances the column. |

At this instant board still references the caller's list; white, castling, and en_passant are immutable Boolean/integer values. |
| <a id="make-move-py-L24"></a>24 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;start, target, promotion = move</code> | Unpacks the move's source square, destination square, and optional promotion kind. These integer values are the native representation used throughout the engine, independent of UCI text. |
| <a id="make-move-py-L25"></a>25 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;board = board&#91;:&#93;  # The copy: the caller's board stays as it was</code> | Copies the entire 64-element board list and rebinds the local name board to that copy. A shallow copy is sufficient because elements are immutable integers. All subsequent board edits affect the copy, so another candidate move can start from the unchanged parent position. |
| <a id="make-move-py-L26"></a>26 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;piece = board&#91;start&#93;</code> | Reads the moving piece from the copied source square before that square is cleared. Its encoded value carries both kind and color; that color must survive promotion. |
| <a id="make-move-py-L27"></a>27 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;is_pawn = piece &amp; 7 == PAWN</code> | Masks off the color and compares the low three bits with PAWN. Bitwise `&` binds more tightly than `==`, so the expression means `(piece & 7) == PAWN`. The resulting Boolean controls en passant and double-push handling. |
| <a id="make-move-py-L28"></a>28 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;distance = abs(target - start)</code> | Measures absolute linear-index distance. A legal pawn double push spans 16 entries and a legal castling king move spans 2. This is not a general chess distance: those values identify special moves only under the valid-move assumptions of their piece-specific conditions. |

| <a id="evaluation-py-L89"></a>89 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;continue</code> | Skips the rest of this iteration for an empty square and continues scanning the board. |
| <a id="evaluation-py-L90"></a>90 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kind = piece &amp; 7</code> | Masks off the color flag to obtain kind 1 through 6. This kind indexes both PIECE_VALUES and TABLES. |
| <a id="evaluation-py-L91"></a>91 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;if kind == PAWN:</code> | Checks whether the current piece is a pawn before adding its square to the structural feature lists. |

Ordinary material and placement scoring still follows for this pawn. |
| <a id="evaluation-py-L93"></a>93 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;if piece &amp; WHITE:</code> | Tests the White color bit. White pieces use the displayed table directly; Black pieces need a vertical rank flip. |
| <a id="evaluation-py-L94"></a>94 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;score += PIECE_VALUES&#91;kind&#93; + TABLES&#91;kind&#93;&#91;square&#93;</code> | Adds the White piece's material and its square bonus. A White knight on c3 receives its 300 material plus KNIGHT_TABLE[42]. |
| <a id="evaluation-py-L95"></a>95 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;else:</code> | Handles a Black piece. Its favorable placement must reduce White's score, using the same table from Black's orientation. |
| <a id="evaluation-py-L96"></a>96 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;# Flip the row for Black: Black's a8 is White's a1</code> | Explanatory source comment: Flip the row for Black: Black's a8 is White's a1 Python does not execute this text. The adjacent code rows explain the actual operations and any qualifications to this description. |
| <a id="evaluation-py-L97"></a>97 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;score -= (</code> | Begins subtracting the Black piece's material plus positional contribution. Parentheses continue this one expression onto the following lines. |
| <a id="evaluation-py-L98"></a>98 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;PIECE_VALUES&#91;kind&#93; + TABLES&#91;kind&#93;&#91;(7 - square // 8) &#42; 8 + square % 8&#93;</code> | Combines material with the vertically mirrored table square. `(7-square//8)*8+square%8` flips the rank and preserves the file: Black's a8 reads White's a1 entry, so both colors receive symmetric placement preferences. |
| <a id="evaluation-py-L99"></a>99 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;)</code> | Closes the multiline subtraction expression. It adds no independent calculation beyond completing the score update. |
| <a id="evaluation-py-L100"></a>100 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;if board.count(BISHOP + WHITE) &gt;= 2:</code> | Checks whether at least two White bishops are present. This is a piece-count approximation to the bishop-pair advantage. |
| <a id="evaluation-py-L101"></a>101 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;score += BISHOP_PAIR</code> | Adds White's 30-centipawn bishop-pair bonus once, even if promotion produced more than two bishops. |
| <a id="evaluation-py-L102"></a>102 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;if board.count(BISHOP) &gt;= 2:</code> | Performs the same bishop-count threshold for Black's uncolored bishop code. |
| <a id="evaluation-py-L103"></a>103 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;score -= BISHOP_PAIR</code> | Subtracts Black's bishop-pair bonus from the White-relative score. |
| <a id="evaluation-py-L104"></a>104 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;score += pawn_terms(pawns)</code> | Adds the net doubled, isolated, and passed-pawn terms calculated from the collected pawn lists. |
| <a id="evaluation-py-L105"></a>105 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;return score if white else -score</code> | Returns White's accumulated score when White moves and its negative when Black moves. A White advantage of 100 becomes -100 if Black is to move; this convention is essential to negamax. |

#### Lines 17 to 27: store()

| Line | Exact source | Detailed explanation |
| ---: | --- | --- |
| <a id="transposition-table-py-L17"></a>17 | <code>def store(key: int, depth: int, score: int, bound: int, move: Move &#124; None, ply: int):</code> | Defines storage of a search result. key identifies the position hash, depth describes search coverage, bound describes score reliability, move improves ordering, and ply normalizes mate distance. |
| <a id="transposition-table-py-L18"></a>18 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;"""Save one search result. A mate score is stored as mate from here."""</code> | One-line docstring for store. It records the function or class contract as text; the following executable lines implement that contract. A docstring does not validate inputs or enforce its stated guarantee. |
| <a id="transposition-table-py-L19"></a>19 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;if score &gt; MATE_SCORE - 1000:</code> | Recognizes a positive mate-like score above 99,000. Ordinary material scores are far smaller, so this band is reserved for mating values. |
| <a id="transposition-table-py-L20"></a>20 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;score += ply</code> | Adds root distance to a winning mate score before storing it. For example, 99995 found at ply 2 becomes 99997, expressing a mate three plies from this position rather than five from the root. |
| <a id="transposition-table-py-L21"></a>21 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;elif score &lt; -MATE_SCORE + 1000:</code> | Recognizes a negative mate-like score below -99,000 using the symmetric threshold. |
| <a id="transposition-table-py-L22"></a>22 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;score -= ply</code> | Subtracts ply from a losing mate score, removing the current root-distance component before caching it. |
| <a id="transposition-table-py-L23"></a>23 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;if len(table) &gt; MAX_ENTRIES:</code> | Checks whether the entry count is already greater than the threshold before insertion. The strict greater-than test permits the dictionary to grow to MAX_ENTRIES+1 before a later store clears it. |
| <a id="transposition-table-py-L24"></a>24 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;table.clear()</code> | Clears the entire cache at once. There is no age, depth, or bucket replacement policy in this Python implementation. |
| <a id="transposition-table-py-L25"></a>25 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;table&#91;key&#93; = (depth, score, bound, move)</code> | Stores or overwrites this key's result unconditionally. A shallower result may replace a deeper one; the depth field later determines whether a score is reusable. |

| <a id="opening-book-py-L28"></a>28 | <code>def book_moves(played: list&#91;str&#93;, white: bool) -&gt; list&#91;str&#93;:</code> | Defines book lookup from the full played UCI sequence and the current side. Matching is by exact move-order prefix, not by board-position transposition. |
| <a id="opening-book-py-L29"></a>29 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;"""Return the book's next moves after the moves played, in random order."""</code> | One-line docstring for book_moves. It records the function or class contract as text; the following executable lines implement that contract. A docstring does not validate inputs or enforce its stated guarantee. |
| <a id="opening-book-py-L30"></a>30 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;moves = &#91;&#93;</code> | Starts the candidate continuation list for this lookup. |
| <a id="opening-book-py-L31"></a>31 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;for side, line in book:</code> | Scans every loaded opening entry, including entries that share the same opening prefix. |
| <a id="opening-book-py-L32"></a>32 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;defends = side == "" or (side == "White") == white</code> | Allows unrestricted entries or entries labeled for the current side. A Black-only defense line can supply Black's moves but cannot supply its White setup moves. |
| <a id="opening-book-py-L33"></a>33 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;if defends and len(line) &gt; len(played) and line&#91;: len(played)&#93; == played:</code> | Requires an allowed side, at least one unplayed token, and exact equality between the stored prefix and the game history. Once a game deviates from every prefix, lookup produces no continuation. |
| <a id="opening-book-py-L34"></a>34 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;moves.append(line&#91;len(played)&#93;)</code> | Appends the next token after the played prefix. The same move can be appended from several entries; duplicates are retained and therefore influence random selection probability. |
| <a id="opening-book-py-L35"></a>35 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;random.shuffle(moves)</code> | Shuffles candidates in place. It changes their order, not their legality, and does not deduplicate repeated moves. |
| <a id="opening-book-py-L36"></a>36 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;return moves</code> | Returns the shuffled UCI strings. legal_book_move() must find a matching native candidate and confirm king safety before any token is played. |

### search.py

| <a id="uci-py-L29"></a>29 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;for text in book_moves(played, position&#91;1&#93;):</code> | Visits matching book continuations in shuffled order. The current side flag enforces side-restricted entries before this loop begins. |
| <a id="uci-py-L30"></a>30 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;for move in generate_moves(position):</code> | Generates native movement candidates for each book token. This repeats generation for multiple tokens but keeps the validation straightforward. |
| <a id="uci-py-L31"></a>31 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;child = make_move(position, move)</code> | Makes each candidate on a copy. In this implementation the copy is made even before checking whether its text matches the book token. |
| <a id="uci-py-L32"></a>32 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;if move_text(move) == text and not in_check(child&#91;0&#93;, position&#91;1&#93;):</code> | Requires both exact UCI text equality and a safe moving king on the child board. Short-circuit and avoids the attack test for text mismatches. A malformed or illegal book token cannot pass merely by appearing in the file. |
| <a id="uci-py-L33"></a>33 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;return move</code> | Returns the first native legal move matching a shuffled candidate. Its tuple is suitable for ordinary engine use and bestmove formatting. |
| <a id="uci-py-L34"></a>34 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;return None</code> | Returns None if no book suggestion is legal and matched. The UCI go branch then calls think(). |

| Line | Exact source | Detailed explanation |

| <a id="uci-py-L37"></a>37 | <code>def main():</code> | Defines the protocol loop. Its body does not execute when uci.py is imported by the verifier; the main guard at the end decides when to run it. |
| <a id="uci-py-L38"></a>38 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;load_book()</code> | Loads optional opening data once before processing input. A missing file leaves the book empty and search remains available. |
| <a id="uci-py-L39"></a>39 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;position = START_POSITION</code> | Starts from the shared initial Position. Later calls to make_move() return copied boards, so this assignment does not consume or mutate the starting position. |
| <a id="uci-py-L40"></a>40 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;played = &#91;&#93;  # The moves of the game, as UCI text</code> | Initializes the complete played-move token list for book-prefix matching. This list is replaced by each position command. |
| <a id="uci-py-L41"></a>41 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;for line in sys.stdin:</code> | Reads stdin one line at a time until the stream closes or a quit command breaks the loop. Waiting for another line blocks the process. The loop is synchronous; there is no separate input thread while think() runs. |
| <a id="uci-py-L42"></a>42 | <code>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;words = line.split()</code> | Splits a line on arbitrary whitespace and removes empty whitespace fields. A trailing newline disappears, and a whitespace-only input line produces an empty list. |

### remus3.bat

Launches the Python UCI entry point through uv on Windows. Source: [remus3.bat](remus3.bat).

#### Lines 1 to 1: Windows launcher

| Line | Exact source | Detailed explanation |
| ---: | --- | --- |
| <a id="remus-bat-L1"></a>1 | <code>uv run uci.py</code> | The Windows command interpreter invokes `uv`, whose `run` subcommand starts `uci.py` in the discovered project Python environment. The relative script name requires the engine folder as the working directory; the batch file does not change directories or use `%~dp0` to anchor the script. stdin and stdout remain available to the chess GUI. This line invokes no chess logic itself. It has no `@echo off`, so command echo behavior depends on the command interpreter context. uv must be installed and available on PATH even though the Python engine modules use only the standard library. |

### piece_square_tables.json

<a id="piece-square-tables-json"></a>

These supplied tables are identical in Remus2 to Remus4. Students type the loader and named assignments, not the values. Each table holds eight ranks of eight centipawn values, rank 8 first and files a to h across. Loading occurs once during module import.
